### T-7301 · Rollback wave-5 · a pending file selection clears on rollback — the chosen file never implies a coming upload
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CV-RB

Steps: 1. Open /settings, expand a group with a file-picker field. 2. Choose a file in the picker but do NOT save or upload. 3. Roll the group back. 4. Read the file picker state and any caption near it.

Expected: The picker shows no file selected after the rollback — the un-saved choice is discarded, not carried forward. A filename still sitting in the picker (implying it will upload on the next save, or surviving a rollback it was never part of) is a FAIL. If no group offers a file picker, N-A.

### T-7302 · Rollback wave-5 · rollback of one group during another group's save-in-flight resolves both honestly — never mixed values
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CV-RB

Steps: 1. Open /settings with two groups editable. 2. Edit group 1 and press its Save; while its save is in flight, edit group 2 and press group 2's rollback. 3. Wait for both to settle, then read both groups' fields verbatim. 4. Reload and read both groups again.

Expected: Each group resolves to its own honest outcome — group 1 shows its saved values, group 2 shows its rolled-back values — and the reload agrees with both. Values crossed between groups, a save overwritten by an unrelated rollback, or a panel that shows one state and reloads into another, is a FAIL. (BN proved the single-group save/rollback race; this is the cross-group serialization seam.)

### T-7303 · Rollback wave-5 · the rollback control itself reads in plain words — revert-to-saved is obvious before clicking
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: READ
Feature: CV-RB

Steps: 1. Open /settings and expand three different groups. 2. Read the rollback control's label (and any hint/title text) in each group verbatim. 3. Check whether the label is identical across all groups or varies per group.

Expected: The label (and any helper hint) makes the meaning obvious to a non-technical reader — "revert my unsaved changes back to the saved values" — with no jargon. An unlabeled icon, a label like "Reset" that could equally mean reset-to-defaults (a different, destructive action), or inconsistent labeling across groups is a FAIL. (Reset-to-defaults vs revert-to-saved are two different blast radii and must never share a label.)

### T-7304 · Rollback wave-5 · a multi-select list's full saved selection set restores — not just the first item
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.

Feature: CV-RB

Expected: A, B, and C are all selected again — the exact saved set. A rollback that restores only the first selected item (or silently drops part of the set) is a FAIL. (BN proved single-select exact restore; this is the multi-select seam.)

### T-7305 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · an empty-string provider response names itself — distinct from malformed and from outage copy
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: READ
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. Open the AI providers panel and trigger a Test probe while watching the response contract. 2. When a probe ever returns an empty-string body (200 with no text, or a verdict call that answers with nothing), read the panel's response wording verbatim. 3. Compare it against the malformed-response copy and the outage copy.

Expected: The empty response is named for what it is — the panel says the provider answered but said nothing, in plain words. It must NOT borrow the malformed-response wording (which implies a shape problem) and must NOT borrow the outage wording (which implies the provider never answered). An empty answer wearing the outage copy would send Saad checking the connection for a provider-side emptiness — the three states stay distinct. If no empty response occurs naturally in the session, the case is recorded N-A with that absence stated; the control copy is the outage wording from BI T-6940.

### T-7306 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · a verdict vetoed before the AI call leaves no cost row — the counter bills only calls that fired
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: READ
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. With the AI loop on and a real provider keyed, note the cost counter's current row count and total. 2. Watch the signal flow until a signal arrives that the guards veto BEFORE the brain is consulted (a guard veto, not an AI call). 3. Read the cost counter again — rows and total — and read the veto's record for any brain-cost attribution.

Expected: The vetoed signal adds NO cost row and the total does not move. A veto is a guard decision with no provider call behind it, so billing nothing is the only honest answer. A phantom row (even $0.0000) attached to a call that never fired is a FAIL — CL proved exactly-once billing for calls that fired; this is the no-call seam. If no guard veto occurs in the session, record N-A with the absence stated.

### T-7307 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · the expanded history row keeps its provider label across a switch — no hot-swap
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. Open the AI verdict/cost history and find a row produced under provider A. 2. Expand that row (open its disclosure/details) and leave it expanded. 3. In another part of the panel switch the active provider from A to B. 4. Return to the history and read the still-expanded row verbatim.

Expected: The expanded row still names provider A everywhere — its provider label, attribution line, and any nested text are frozen at render time. A row that hot-swaps to "provider B" because the global active provider changed is a FAIL — history describes the past, not the current selection. (BI proved attribution across a switch on the row list; this is the expanded-disclosure seam.)

### T-7308 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · the loop-toggle flip mid-scheduled-scan ends the scan honestly — no stranded half-billed row
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. With the AI loop ON and a scheduled scan running (its in-flight indicator visible), flip the loop toggle OFF mid-scan. 2. Read the scan row's final state, the cost counter, and the loop status verbatim. 3. Flip the loop back ON and read the next scheduled scan's state.

Expected: The interrupted scan ends in an honest state — labeled stopped or incomplete with the reason named (loop disabled), never "Completed" and never stuck in-flight. The cost counter shows only what was actually billed: a call that never fired bills nothing; a call that completed before the flip keeps exactly its row. A stranded row marked in-flight forever, or a row that silently vanishes, is a FAIL. The next scan after re-enabling runs fresh — no ghost of the interrupted one.

### T-7309 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · a guard veto and an AI verdict on the same signal name their authorities — never blended
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.

Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. Watch the signal flow until a signal is BOTH vetoed by the guards AND advised on by the brain (the brain ran before the veto, or the advisory arrived with the veto already in force). 2. Read the signal card, the guard veto text, and the AI verdict block verbatim. 3. Check which authority the final state (trade/no-trade) is attributed to.

Expected: The two authorities speak in their own voices and the final decision is attributed to the guards — the veto decides, the brain advised. The verdict block must NOT be phrased as the reason for the veto, and the veto copy must NOT borrow the verdict's recommendation as its justification. A blended sentence ("Vetoed because the AI said…") or a veto that silently inherits the verdict's wording is a FAIL — Saad must always know which brain made the call. If no veto+verdict coincidence occurs in the session, record N-A with the absence stated.

Time a verdict so it lands within ~60 seconds of the hour boundary (or find a naturally occurring boundary verdict). 3. Read the per-hour usage count on both sides of the boundary and the verdict's cost row timestamp verbatim.

The per-hour counter resets to the correct count after the rollover, never to a stale or negative number. If no verdict lands near a boundary in the session, record N-A with the absence stated.

### T-7312 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · the sealed AI profile's caps render unchanged after a provider switch — no drift
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. Open the AI profile and read the sealed caps verbatim: MAX PER DAY, MAX PER HOUR, COOLDOWN, the "Sealed" marker, and the "Closed" badge. 2. Switch the active provider to the other slot. 3. Read the profile's caps again verbatim. 4. Switch back and read them a third time.

Expected: Every number is identical before and after the switch — MAX PER DAY 6, MAX PER HOUR 2, COOLDOWN 1 hour, Sealed present, "Closed" badge present. The provider switch changes the provider, not the profile: a cap that drifts (a number changes, the Sealed marker drops, the badge disappears) is a FAIL. The sealed profile is a promise; a UI that rewrites the promise on a switch is breaking it quietly.

### T-7313 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · a tunnel reload between verdicts re-renders the same verdict — no blank, no duplicate
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.

Method: READ
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. With the AI loop on, let a verdict land and read it verbatim (text, timestamp, provider). 2. Reload the page (tunnel rotation counts — any reload between verdicts). 3. Read the verdict area verbatim after the reload settles. 4. Compare: same verdict once, or an honest state.

Expected: The verdict area shows exactly the same verdict exactly once — text, timestamp, and provider identical to step 1 — or an honest loading state that resolves to it. A blank area that never resolves, a doubled verdict (the same text twice), or a DIFFERENT verdict silently substituted for the remembered one is a FAIL. Reloads must not rewrite history. (CL proved "last checked" survives a rotation; this is the verdict-body seam.)

### T-7314 · AI-provider paths wave-4 · two consecutive verdicts keep their disclosures attached — reasoning never cross-wires
**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: CW-AI

Steps: 1. With the AI loop on, wait until two verdicts arrive close together (within seconds). 2. Expand the FIRST verdict's reasoning disclosure and read it verbatim. 3. Expand the SECOND verdict's reasoning disclosure and read it verbatim. 4. Collapse and re-expand the first and read it again.

Expected: Each disclosure belongs to its own verdict — the first expansion names the first verdict's reasoning, the second names the second's, and re-expanding the first still shows the first. A disclosure that shows the sibling verdict's reasoning (cross-wired), one that collapses when the other opens without the user collapsing it, or an expansion that loses its content on the second open is a FAIL. Two verdicts in flight must never share a mouth. If no close pair arrives in the session, record N-A with the absence stated.

## Appendix I — Issue registry (every issue ever found, with live status)

Status legend — one of: **FIXED** (both gates passed + at least one re-verification, never on a claim alone), **NOT-A-BUG** (investigated, proven by design/transient), **FIX-WAVE-RUNNING** (fix team dispatched, verdict pending), **OPEN** (no wave yet, or waiting on re-verification), **NOT-FIXED** (a wave attempted and the issue survives). Re-verification log under each entry records EVERY later check with date + result.

### ISS-001 —001001001001 /trading "dead page" (2026-09-26 ~23:15 PDT)
- Observed (exact): when the status API failed, the page painted zero cards with zero message — a blank dead page, no explanation, no recovery path.
- Where: /trading (root cockpit trading view).
- Severity: blocker.
- Status: FIXED (2026-09-26 ~23:15 PDT).
- Root cause: no failure branch — the render path assumed the status API always answers.
- Fix: an honest "Couldn't reach the trading engine" card + a Retry button now renders on status-API failure (canonical trading_mvp tree).
- UI gate: PASS — in-browser confirmation on /trading.
- Backend gate: PASS — independent verifier.
- Re-verification log: 2026-09-26 ~23:15 PDT — independent verifiers + in-browser confirmation, all resolved.

But the operator halt (POST /v1/admin/kill) only blocks NEW entries — it never closes open positions (position-closing lives only in the separate KILL_FLATTEN tier).

- Status: FIXED.
- Root cause: copy written for a fuller halt than the operator route implements.

Resume from the risk panel."; matching explainer fixes. Endpoint needed no change (intentional — design intent confirmed from code).
- UI gate: independently verified PASS (served bytes: corrected strings present, overstating strings zero). Backend gate: wiring intact (4 refs to /v1/admin/kill), no POST ever fired during verification. No latency impact (text-only).

### ISS-061 —061061061061 Envelope validator goes SILENT when its backend call fails/hangs (found 2026-09-27 ~03:17 PDT, batch 4 case T-5328 — FAIL)
- Observed (exact): during a backend-degraded window (~09:57–10:01 UTC), 7 Validate presses (automation click x4, keyboard Enter, visual pointer click, click+instant capture) with 2 input types produced NOTHING — no verdict, no failure message; #envelope-verdict stayed empty (confirmed via HTML). Case T-5328 expected: "Every press produces a verdict or a failure message -- never nothing."
- Where: / (root cockpit), envelope validator block.
- Severity: major (a validation control that swallows presses and hangs silently — the exact "vibe-coded" failure mode; a trader gets no answer and no error).
- Status: FIXED (mini fix-wave 2026-09-27 ~03:20 PDT — 1 fix team + 2 independent verifiers, none wrote the fix).
- Root cause (confirmed by code inspection): (1) timeout race — `htmx.config.timeout = 20000` sat in a non-deferred inline script that ran BEFORE the deferred htmx 2.0.4 load, so the guard silently skipped and the vendored default `timeout:0` = hang-forever; (2) total-silence path — the instant local verdict + failure repaints live only in deferred `/static/form-errors.js`; if that file failed/hung during degradation, no submit listener registered -> htmx POST hangs with no timeout -> absolute silence (the 7-press observation).
- Fix (canonical tree only): `templates/base.html` — dead timeout line replaced with `tvexApplyHtmxTimeout()` applied immediately AND on DOMContentLoaded (idempotent, try/catch); added an inline envelope-fallback block (addEventListener only: submit + htmx:sendError/htmx:timeout/htmx:responseError listeners) painting into #envelope-verdict, standing down when `window.__tvexFormErrorsLoaded` is set; `form-errors.js?v=20260927a->20260927b`. `static/form-errors.js` — re-asserts the 20s cap at IIFE top, sets `window.__tvexFormErrorsLoaded = true` at end. No validation semantics changed (valid stays valid, invalid stays invalid with same reasons).
- UI gate: PASS (independent verifier, served-bytes — no failure-triggered DOM captured, subagents cannot drive a live browser: fallback messages "Checking the envelope — one moment…" and "The validator couldn't finish its check — try again in a moment." present verbatim; all local-verdict wordings byte-identical to baseline; node --check clean; zero banned words).
- Backend gate: PASS (independent verifier, read-only: diff vs pristine baselines = exactly the fix; timeout wiring real — immediate call is parsing-time no-op, DOMContentLoaded is the true applicator, IIFE-top re-assertion is belt-and-braces; stand-down logic confirmed; live POST /v1/ui/strategies/validate -> 200 "Schema PASS" / "Schema REJECTED"; app/main.py untouched; port 8123 never touched; no Telegram sends).
- Latency impact: zero (only a config assignment on DOMContentLoaded, one boolean flag, document-level listeners that no-op unless the envelope form errors; the 20s cap only shortens the worst case from infinite hang to a 20s failure message).
- Live-DOM failure-repaint re-verification for T-5328 queued for the next natural degraded window during later batches (same pattern as T-5274).
- Note: pre-existing `mode:'paper'` inside localEnvelopeVerdict deliberately untouched (contract vocabulary, out of scope). Sibling observation from T-5329 — malformed-JSON verdict reason is a raw parser message, not trader-plain words; still PASS per the case, flagged for a future plain-words pass.

- Latency impact: negligible (one string comparison per validation per layer).
- ⚠️ STANDING-RULE CONFLICT — real paper mode found in the engine (Saad's decision required, queued 2026-09-27 ~04:15 PDT): "paper" is not just an unvalidated string — the engine contains a WORKING paper-trading capability, deliberately left untouched by the fix team. Evidence (canonical tree): app/config.py L286–292 VENUE_ROUTING — `Set "default": "paper" to route fills to the honest paper simulator`; app/live_guards.py L105–150 — is_paper_venue(), venue_mode() returning "paper", order_mode() returning "paper"/"demo"/"live"; app/webhook_drain.py L204 — live code path for a paper venue ("Paper trading is dead (owner order 2026-09-22): never auto-execute"); app/settings_routes.py L533/666 — mode="paper" confirm rows; app/confirms.jsonl — hundreds of live confirm rows with "mode": "paper"; app/models.py L188–194 — mode: Literal["paper","live"] = "live" ("paper" was the default until 2026-09-25). Webhook ingress refuses paper-mode alerts today, so no alert can activate paper trading — but the simulator + venue-routing + paper/demo labeling machinery still exists, directly contradicting Saad's standing rule that no paper/demo mode will ever exist. Saad decided 2026-09-27 ~04:17 PDT ("I give you authority on those too" — authority extended to the paper-mode conflict): FULL REMOVAL. ### ISS-097 — successful "Draft ticket" renders no confirmation → silent duplicate drafts (found 2026-09-27 ~12:39 PDT, UI-gate anomaly)
- Observed (exact): clicking "Draft ticket" with a valid note rendered NO transient confirmation in the AI copilot panel; each click silently created a draft server-side. The UI-gate run's 4 single clicks (no double-clicks) produced 4 near-duplicate "UI short" drafts — drf-5e0f281d65ba, drf-6785189ed46b, drf-2a929fbcdbce, drf-b15fb10bc071 — because nothing visible told the operator the first draft was already saved. (Note: the ISS-096 fix disables the button during the POST via hx-disabled-elt, but the operator still gets no positive feedback that the draft landed.)
- Where: home AI STANCE & COPILOT → Draft ticket flow.
- Severity: major (silent duplicate creation; the operator cannot tell success happened).
- Status: FIXED — backend gate PASS 9/9 (served bytes from pid 14288, 2026-09-27 ~12:46 PDT). Root cause: the draft form fired a multi-second model call (`_p3_render`) with zero in-flight feedback; the "Saved" note rendered below the long Pine block; nothing stopped an identical second click from silently persisting another draft. Fix (3 parts): (1) `hx-indicator='#draft-working'` with the working note "Drafting… this can take a few seconds while the model renders Pine." (role=status, CSS in night-desk.css, ?v=20260927b); (2) top confirmation "Draft saved — saved to SmartTrade drafts as drf-… — pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts." as the first element of the success result (canonical draft header byte-identical; ISS-096 REJECTED contract untouched); (3) duplicate guard — normalized fingerprint recorded in memory; an identical click within 120s returns the existing drf-id with "(This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.)" before the model call, so a duplicate costs zero provider calls. Proof: verifier B's staged live double-submit — POST 1 → drf-2b8e6ef439cc, POST 2 → same id + "no duplicate was created", staged drafts 0→1, staged ledger +1 row (POST 1 only); live honesty ledger unchanged; zero provider calls against live 8124. RE-DIAGNOSIS (2026-09-27 ~12:56 PDT, coordinator + 2 independent agents, live pid 14288 untouched):
- R1 (step-1 confirmation never rendered): TEST-ARTIFACT — no bug, no fix needed. uvicorn.log: `POST /v1/ui/ai/draft 200 OK` completed within ~1 min; the confirmation HTML sits on every successful DRAFT persist path (app/main.py ~12496), so since the draft was created, the confirmation was in the emitted bytes. The 19:47 request took the model-failure → template-fallback path (tens of seconds); the operator reloaded the page mid-flight, aborting the htmx request client-side — a reloaded panel contains no confirmation (it exists only in the POST response fragment), so "never rendered" is the expected outcome of the reload. Zero exceptions/500s/truncated responses in the window; no overlapping draft POSTs.
- R2 (step-2 confirmation rendered): confirms the render path works — same code path as step 1; the only difference was the client consumed the response.
- R3 (guard not exercised in-window): GUARD VERIFIED WORKING — staged copy (port 18298, ledgers redirected, zero provider calls): POST 1 "REDIAG test: ADA long on 15m support bounce" → drf-924249cbf142; POST 2 identical text 4.25s later → SAME drf-id + "(This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.)" + "Pine render skipped for the duplicate"; staged drafts 0→1 exactly; POST 2 added zero ledger rows (guard runs before the model call). The live UI gate's step-2 duplicate was correct by-design outside-window behavior.
- Files changed: NONE. Backend gate: PASS (staged duplicate-guard test; live ledgers unchanged; zero provider calls against live 8124).
DIAGNOSIS + FIX WAVE FINAL REPORT (2026-09-27 ~13:28 PDT, coordinator + 6 workers):
- ROOT CAUSE (3 independent teams agreed, server innocent): the ~20s synchronous Pine render inside POST /v1/ui/ai/draft raced htmx's 20s XHR timeout (htmx.config.timeout = 20000, set by tvexApplyHtmxTimeout() in templates/base.html, introduced 19:40:31 UTC — minutes before the failing tests). Template-fallback renders land at/over 20s → XHR times out client-side → confirmation never renders, "Drafting…" indicator sticks. Path correlation 3/3: 19:47 + 19:56 template-fallback (~20s) never rendered; 19:51 model render (~19s) rendered fine. All server 200s, no exceptions. Curl: origin framing clean (single-string HTMLResponse, explicit Content-Length, no chunking). Real htmx 2.0.4 in jsdom swaps the failing bytes cleanly — timing is the only systematic differential.
- FIX (canonical tree, app/main.py only, markers FIX-ISS097/FIX-ISS097b): Pine render extracted to _render_pine_html() (line 12263); POST now returns in ~0.02s with <strong>Draft saved</strong> + drf-id + byte-identical <h4>AI DRAFT (DRAFT TEXT, ZERO ORDERS)</h4> header + <div id='pine-…'> placeholder (hx-get='/v1/ui/ai/draft/pine?job=…' hx-trigger='load' hx-swap='outerHTML') + "Retry Pine render" button; drafts stashed in bounded app.state._pine_pending (cap 20). New GET /v1/ui/ai/draft/pine?job=… (line 12580) runs the render async; expired (>1h) jobs get an honest "expired — press Draft ticket again" (200). Duplicate guard + ISS-088 persist included; ISS-096 REJECTED contract untouched (verified).
- LIVE: pid 1696, started 2026-09-27 20:18:54 UTC (75s after the fix write) — running the fixed code. keepalive incident (stale .keepalive.lock, "TUNNEL FAILED TO START") handled by the coordinator; keepalive.sh not edited. Current tunnel https://ee6072c5d59c2c.lhr.life; bookmark unchanged.
- BACKEND GATE: PASS — 6 checks x 2 independent verifiers on served bytes from pid 1696: (1) fast confirmation 200 in ~0.025s with Draft saved + drf- + pinejob- + hx-trigger='load'; (2) async Pine render 200 with <pre class='pine-draft'>; (3) duplicate guard — both verifiers' first re-POSTs missed due to CONCURRENT-TEST ARTIFACT (single global slot app.state._copilot_draft_last clobbered by the other verifier's POST 2s earlier), adjudicated PASS via isolated live probes (identical re-POST 3–5s later → same drf-id + "no duplicate was created", twice; verifier B's own 3rd/4th POSTs deduped). Design caveat (not a defect): single-slot means two operators interleaving drafts can defeat the guard — fine for single-operator TEST cockpit, documented in code. (4) REJECTED contract intact; (5) expired job honest 200; (6) canonical header byte-identical. Ledgers: honesty 33→35 (verifiers' own renders), jev 7→7, no TestClient pollution. Provider spend ~$0.005 disclosed ($0.001819 + $0.001403 + ~$0.002).
FOLLOW-THROUGH FIX WAVE FINAL REPORT (2026-09-27 ~13:42 PDT, coordinator + 5 workers):
- R1 — Pine GET timeout mismatch (real bug): GET /v1/ui/ai/draft/pine takes ~25–30s wall time (model → non-JSON → template fallback), but htmx.config.timeout = 20000 aborts it at 20s in a real browser → placeholder sits unfilled forever; server-side render dies on a dead connection.
- R2 — success path cleared the textarea (design bug breaking the guard's contract): _draft_out was called WITHOUT preserve on success, so a natural second click submitted text="" → 422 note — the duplicate guard was UNREACHABLE in the natural UI flow.
- R3 — click-2 160s hang (environmental + robustness gap): click 2's POST got a 200 in ~0.01s server-side (uvicorn.log line 24484) — the browser never settled the XHR. Decompiled htmx 2.0.4 proves the indicator clears on every terminal path incl. ontimeout, so a 160s-visible indicator forces htmx.config.timeout === 0 in that tab: the ISS-061 inline setter silently skipped at parse time (vendored htmx loads with defer, window.htmx undefined), leaving a stale tab; a localhost.run tunnel stall then hung the XHR with no client abort. Server-side stall RULED OUT (Pine GET runs in FastAPI's threadpool, draft POST is async, guard is pure dict lookups, no shared locks).
- FIXES (canonical tree, markers FIX-ISS097f): F1 — hx-request='{"timeout":120000}' on the Pine placeholder div AND Retry button (120s covers the 25–30s render); F2 — preserve=text on the SUCCESS path (text stays, guard reachable by natural second click; ISS-096 REJECTED untouched); F3 — Retry button starts display:none, revealed ONLY on htmx:timeout/htmx:responseError of the Pine GET; F4 — timeout setter hardened (re-asserts on htmx:load + self-clearing setInterval backstop until window.htmx exists; 20s cap now guaranteed on every tab); F5 — all prior contracts kept (fast POST ~0.01s, byte-identical header, guard logic + 120s window + note, ISS-088 persist).
- LIVE: pid 5754, restarted via bash keepalive.sh check after the fix write; coordinator verified tree + served bytes directly (fix team's handoff was malformed).
- BACKEND GATE: PASS — 10/10 x 2 independent verifiers on served bytes from pid 5754: fast confirmation, placeholder timeout attrs, textarea preserved, guard back-to-back (check-4 note: first attempts missed due to the known single-global-slot interference between the two concurrent verifiers — honestly reported, adjudicated PASS; documented design limitation), empty→422, REJECTED intact, expired job honest, hardened setter in served HTML, no duplicate drafts, ledgers unchanged (38→38, 7→7 md5-verified). Zero provider calls by verifiers.
UI GATE: PASS (2026-09-27 ~13:49 PDT, fresh tab on live tunnel dcdc869379a030.lhr.life — tunnel rotated mid-setup, reconnected clean): click 1 → "Draft saved — saved to SmartTrade drafts as drf-cfa51c96f9c9 — pick it up under SmartTrade → MCP drafts." in ~2–4s + exact "AI DRAFT (DRAFT TEXT, ZERO ORDERS)" header, TEXT PRESERVED in the box; Pine placeholder filled with the Pine block in ~35s, Retry button NEVER visible; click 2 (~55s later, no retype) → SAME drf-id + "(This is your existing draft from your last identical request — no duplicate was created.)" in ~2s, no hang, no second drf-id; MCP DRAFTS exactly one new draft. Note: htmx.config.timeout console check could not be executed by the automation (no JS capability) — behaviorally all htmx requests completed in seconds with no hangs, and the served bytes were backend-verified. ### T-7348 · Notification history wave-7 · a row's relative age and its absolute timestamp agree across an auto-refresh tick — the age ticks forward, the timestamp never moves

Method: STATE-OBSERVATION
Feature: DA-NH

Expected: The absolute timestamp never changes between ticks, the relative age ticks forward honestly (never backward, never a forward jump that contradicts the timestamp), and the age always equals the desk clock minus the row's absolute timestamp within a tick. An age label reading "just now" for a row whose absolute timestamp is ten minutes old, or an age that jumps backward after the tick, is a FAIL.

Method: INTERACTION
Feature: DB-RB

Turn the toggle ON, change the conditional field to value Y, turn the toggle OFF again (the field is hidden with an unsaved edit), and roll the group back. 3. Turn the toggle ON and read the conditional field verbatim.

Expected: The field reads X — the saved value. A rollback that leaves Y live because "the field wasn't visible when it reverted", or that leaves the field in any state other than the saved one, is a FAIL. (T-7003 proved revealed fields roll back with their parent toggle — this is the hidden-at-rollback seam.)

### T-7368 · Error/empty states wave-6 · back-to-back failures on one region replace the error line — never stack two failure lines

**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: DC-ERR

Steps: 1. Find a region that renders failure lines (the /history balance block's honesty lines are the natural candidate). 2. Observe two naturally distinct failures in succession on the same region (two failed refreshes, or a failure followed by a differently-worded failure) and count the failure lines after each. 3. Read both lines verbatim: the newest describes the newest failure; the older one is gone, not stacked above it.

Expected: Exactly one failure line per region at any time — the second failure REPLACES the first, the way the preview feedback's replace-not-stack rule works. Two failure lines stacked on the same region (contradictory copy, "STALE" and "could not refresh" both showing, one describing a failure that is no longer current) is a FAIL. (T-5159 proved replace-not-stack for preview feedback; T-7340 proved it for save-failure lines — this is the honest-region line twin.)

A failure injected as a plain, unannounced div — invisible to a screen reader or to a keyboard user whose focus is elsewhere — is a FAIL. (T-5147's lineage gave the preview feedback role="status"; T-7366 gave rollback confirmations a live region — failure lines deserve the same contract.)

### T-7370 · Error/empty states wave-6 · a POST in flight during a natural tunnel rotation blames the connection — never the service

Feature: DC-ERR

### T-7378 · Tunnel-outage wave-5 · a strategy toggle flipped just before a natural rotation holds the engine-agreed state after recovery — no visual rollback on the re-render

**UI-ONLY:** every step through the live browser UI exactly as Saad sees it — clicks, typing, scrolling, keyboard. No backend, no code, no terminals, no API calls, no cheating.
**BACKEND-CHECK:** a separate agent team independently verifies behind the scenes that the action truly took effect as intended — the UI claiming success is never proof on its own.
Method: INTERACTION
Feature: DD-TNL

Steps: 1. On /strategies on a healthy tunnel, flip one strategy's toggle (under the standing approval authority) and confirm the UI confirms the new state; copy the toggle state verbatim. 2. When a natural rotation hits immediately after, keep the page open through the outage render. 3. After recovery, reload and copy the toggle state again; flip the toggle back to its original state and confirm the restore.

Expected: The post-recovery render agrees with the engine — the toggle shows the state it was flipped to before the drop, never rolled back to the pre-flip look by the outage re-render. A toggle that silently reverts its visual state during the outage render (while the engine kept the change) is a FAIL. (T-7128 proved a toggle pressed DURING an outage refuses and stays put — this is the pre-drop success twin: the press landed, the outage must not un-land it.) The toggle is restored to its original state at the end of the case.
